### fep-096: Integral Fluctuation Theorem

Family: `path-space-stochastic-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep096.FEP096.fep096_integralFluctuation_theorem`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/path_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP096`.

Invariant: The forward expectation of exponential negative pointwise entropy production equals one for normalized finite full-support forward and reverse path laws.

### fep-097: Finite Jarzynski Equality

Family: `path-space-stochastic-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `conditional_proxy`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep097.FEP097.fep097_finiteJarzynski_equality`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/path_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP097`.

Invariant: A normalized finite exponential-work protocol has work average equal to the exponential of negative inverse temperature times free-energy difference.

Assumptions: HasJarzynskiNormalization explicitly packages positive beta and the identity that the expectation of exp(-beta times (work minus free-energy difference)) equals one. The theorem algebraically extracts Jarzynski's equality from this normalization; it does not derive that premise from microscopic reversibility.

Non-vacuity: The actual nonconstant Boolean Crooks work witness satisfies the normalization at beta=1 and deltaFreeEnergy=0. Positive beta excludes the zero inverse-temperature parameter boundary; no physical temperature or unit identification is inferred without an explicit convention.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep097_finiteJarzynski_equality with the explicit beta, work, free-energy, and normalization certificate.

```lean
    (work : Path → ℝ)
```

### fep-099: Reversible-Chain One-Step KL Dissipation

Family: `path-space-stochastic-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep099.FEP099.fep099_reversibleChain_oneStep_KL_dissipation`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/path_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP099`.

Invariant: One strictly positive reversible finite Markov step cannot increase KL to its stationary law; the identity transition saturates equality and an explicit irreversible path protocol has positive entropy production.

Assumptions: State is finite and nonempty, actual and stationary laws have full support, every transition atom is positive, and detailed balance supplies stationarity. The result is one-step dissipation, not strict contraction for every nonidentity kernel or a continuous-time entropy-production theorem.

Non-vacuity: A strictly positive symmetric mixing kernel supplies an instance of the main dissipation theorem. The separately proved identity equality lies outside its positive-transition class on multi-state carriers. Unequal full-support Boolean path laws prove positive path divergence; the existing stationary biased three-cycle separately proves nonreversibility and positive production at every positive finite horizon.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep099_reversibleChain_oneStep_KL_dissipation, fep099_reversibleEquality_boundary, and fep099_irreversiblePositiveProduction_witness.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep099_reversibleChain_oneStep_KL_dissipation
    [Nonempty State]
    (actual stationary : FiniteLaw State)
    (kernel : FiniteKernel State State)
    (hActual : ∀ state, 0 < actual state)
    (hStationary : ∀ state, 0 < stationary state)
    (hKernel : ∀ source target, 0 < kernel source target)
    (hReversible : IsReversible stationary kernel) :
    finiteKL (kernel.predictive actual) stationary ≤
      finiteKL actual stationary
```

### fep-100: Categorical Fisher Positivity on Simplex Tangents

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `conditional_proxy`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep100.FEP100.fep100_categoricalFisher_simplexTangent_positivity`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP100`.

Invariant: The inherited Fisher metric is strictly positive on every certified nonzero categorical simplex tangent under full support, while a duplicated-score model exhibits an explicit nonzero null direction.

Assumptions: CategoricalFisherCarrier packages a full-support law, an existing ScoreModel, and exact metric and lowering-map representations on simplex tangents. The general positivity theorem depends on this certificate. A separate genuine Fin 2 categorical construction proves that its one-dimensional simplex tangent space is spanned by (1,-1) and is positive definite there; no claim is made that every arbitrary score parameterization is full rank.

Non-vacuity: The explicit Fin 2 categorical law and nonzero tangent (1,-1) have Fisher energy exactly four, and every Fin 2 simplex tangent is a multiple of that direction. Duplicated score coordinates separately give a nonzero tangent with zero Fisher energy.

### fep-101: Fisher Pullback under Reparameterization

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep101.FEP101.fep101_fisherPullback_reparameterization`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP101`.

Invariant: Pulling the existing finite Fisher metric through a composite Jacobian equals successive pullback, and every such pullback is positive semidefinite.

Assumptions: Outcome and coordinate carriers are finite and Jacobians are finite real matrices. No invertibility is needed for pullback or semidefiniteness, so a rank-deficient chart may retain null directions; positive definiteness is not claimed here.

Non-vacuity: Nonidentity rectangular Jacobians exercise genuine reparameterization, while a rank-deficient matrix demonstrates why the conclusion is nonnegative rather than strictly positive.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep101_fisherPullback_reparameterization and fep101_fisherPullback_nonnegative on the inherited ScoreModel Fisher carrier.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep101_fisherPullback_reparameterization
    (model : ScoreModel Outcome d)
    (outer : Matrix (Fin d) (Fin k) ℝ)
    (inner : Matrix (Fin k) (Fin d) ℝ)
    (left right : Fin d → ℝ) :
    pullbackMetric model (outer * inner) left right =
      pullbackMetric model outer (inner.mulVec left) (inner.mulVec right)
```

### fep-103: Natural-Gradient Equivariance under an Invertible Full-Rank Chart

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep103.FEP103.fep103_naturalGradient_equivariance`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP103`.

Invariant: Under invertible Fisher and chart matrices, the natural gradient transports equivariantly so lowering the chart-coordinate vector yields the pulled-back covector.

Assumptions: The finite ScoreModel Fisher matrix and square chart Jacobian each carry an Invertible instance. Rank-deficient metrics or charts are excluded because the natural-gradient and chart-coordinate inverses would not be uniquely defined. The supporting weighted Cauchy–Schwarz theorem requires positive right energy.

Non-vacuity: Nonidentity invertible charts change vector coordinates while preserving the lowered covector law; the proof spike also excludes a zero-energy right vector before deriving its finite weighted inequality.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep103_naturalGradient_equivariance and fep103_weightedCauchySchwarz_proofSpike with both invertibility boundaries.

```lean
theorem fep103_naturalGradient_equivariance
    (model : ScoreModel Outcome d) [Invertible (fisherMatrix model)]
```

### fep-104: Mirror-Descent Three-Point Identity

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep104.FEP104.fep104_mirrorDescent_threePoint_identity`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP104`.

Invariant: The algebraic Bregman expression associated with supplied potential and gradient data obeys the exact three-point identity used by mirror descent.

Assumptions: The coordinate carrier is finite and potential and gradient are arbitrary supplied functions. The identity is algebraic and assumes neither convexity nor that the gradient is the derivative of the potential; those extra properties are required before interpreting every term as a nonnegative divergence or an algorithmic descent guarantee.

Non-vacuity: For Fin1 quadratic potential x0^2/2 and true gradient x0, the points left=2,middle=1,right=0 give divergence difference 2-1/2-1/2=1 and pairing1. This exercises the identity without asserting convexity or derivative compatibility for arbitrary supplied data.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep104_mirrorDescent_threePoint_identity for arbitrary finite-dimensional potential and gradient data.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep104_mirrorDescent_threePoint_identity
    (potential : (Fin d → ℝ) → ℝ)
    (gradient : (Fin d → ℝ) → Fin d → ℝ)
    (left middle right : Fin d → ℝ) :
    bregmanDivergence potential gradient left right -
        bregmanDivergence potential gradient left middle -
        bregmanDivergence potential gradient middle right =
      coordinatePairing
        (fun coordinate ↦
          gradient middle coordinate - gradient right coordinate)
        (fun coordinate ↦ left coordinate - middle coordinate)
```

### fep-105: Bregman Pythagorean Law for an Affine Information Projection

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `conditional_proxy`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep105.FEP105.fep105_affineProjection_bregmanPythagorean`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP105`.

Invariant: An affine projection certificate yields exact Bregman Pythagorean splitting, and a nonnegative residual divergence makes the certified projection a minimizer over the maintained affine set.

Assumptions: AffineBregmanProjection supplies membership and gradient-difference orthogonality for every candidate in a named Set; no IsAffine or convexity predicate is required. Splitting holds under that certificate. The minimization comparison additionally assumes residual Bregman nonnegativity for the selected candidate; obtaining a minimizer over the whole set requires this for all candidates. Existence, uniqueness, derivative compatibility and convexity are not derived.

Non-vacuity: Candidates distinct from the projection can have a positive residual term, producing a strict objective gap; zero residual gives the exact equality boundary without proving uniqueness.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep105_affineProjection_bregmanPythagorean and fep105_affineProjection_minimizes with orthogonality and residual nonnegativity explicit.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep105_affineProjection_bregmanPythagorean
    (potential : (Fin d → ℝ) → ℝ)
    (gradient : (Fin d → ℝ) → Fin d → ℝ)
    (affineSet : Set (Fin d → ℝ))
    (base projection candidate : Fin d → ℝ)
    (hProjection : AffineBregmanProjection potential gradient affineSet
      base projection)
    (hCandidate : candidate ∈ affineSet) :
    bregmanDivergence potential gradient candidate base =
      bregmanDivergence potential gradient candidate projection +
        bregmanDivergence potential gradient projection base
```

### fep-106: Replicator–Natural-Gradient Equivalence

Family: `information-geometry-and-geometric-optimization`. Disposition: `conditional_proxy`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep106.FEP106.fep106_replicator_naturalGradient_equivalence`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_optimization.py`; canonical namespace `FEP106`.

Invariant: On a certified categorical Fisher carrier, the replicator vector is the natural gradient of centered fitness and has zero total mass.

Assumptions: CategoricalFisherCarrier supplies full support and the exact bridge from the existing ScoreModel lowering map to categorical coordinates. The result is an instantaneous finite-simplex vector-field identity, not positivity preservation or convergence of a time-integrated replicator flow.

Non-vacuity: The explicit full-support Fin 2 categorical law with nonconstant fitness yields a nonzero mass-conserving replicator vector and satisfies the inherited natural-gradient predicate; constant fitness produces the exact zero field boundary.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep106_replicator_naturalGradient_equivalence and fep106_replicator_massConservation on the inherited Fisher carrier, plus fep106_twoCategorical_nonzero_replicator_witness on the concrete Fin 2 model.

This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

```lean
theorem fep106_replicator_naturalGradient_equivalence
    (carrier : CategoricalFisherCarrier d) (fitness : Fin d → ℝ) :
    IsNaturalGradient carrier.model
      (fun coordinate ↦ fitness coordinate - meanFitness carrier.law fitness)
      (replicatorVector carrier.law fitness)
```

### fep-107: Product-Agent Generative Law

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep107.FEP107.fep107_productAgent_generative_mass`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP107`.

Invariant: Independent product priors and product observation kernels yield a normalized joint kernel whose generative mass factors exactly into the two agent-level generative masses.

Assumptions: Both state and observation carriers are finite and each component law and kernel is normalized. Independence is built into FiniteLaw.product and the productKernel coordinate definition; the theorem does not infer independence from an arbitrary coupled multiagent law.

Non-vacuity: Nondegenerate component priors and nonconstant component kernels make all four coordinates contribute to joint mass while product-kernel normalization still requires summing over every paired observation.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep107_productAgent_generative_mass and fep107_productKernel_normalized on arbitrary finite component laws and kernels.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep107_productAgent_generative_mass
    (priorLeft : FiniteLaw State₁) (priorRight : FiniteLaw State₂)
    (kernelLeft : FiniteKernel State₁ Observation₁)
    (kernelRight : FiniteKernel State₂ Observation₂)
    (stateLeft : State₁) (stateRight : State₂)
    (observationLeft : Observation₁) (observationRight : Observation₂) :
    (FEP.CollectiveInference.productKernel kernelLeft kernelRight).joint
        (FiniteLaw.product priorLeft priorRight)
        ((stateLeft, stateRight), (observationLeft, observationRight)) =
      kernelLeft.joint priorLeft (stateLeft, observationLeft) *
        kernelRight.joint priorRight (stateRight, observationRight)
```

### fep-108: Additive Collective Variational Free Energy

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep108.FEP108.fep108_collectiveVFE_additive`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP108`.

Invariant: Variational free energy of independent product laws with additive cost is exactly the sum of the two agent-level free energies.

Assumptions: State carriers are finite, both reference factors have full support, actual and reference joint laws are products, and joint cost is additive. The result does not discard a dependence or interaction term for a coupled law or nonadditive cost.

Non-vacuity: Unequal actual and reference factors with nonconstant component costs make both KL and expectation terms contribute, while the separate product-cost theorem isolates the expectation part of additivity.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep108_collectiveVFE_additive and fep108_productCost_additive with both reference-support hypotheses.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep108_collectiveVFE_additive
    (actualLeft referenceLeft : FiniteLaw State₁)
    (actualRight referenceRight : FiniteLaw State₂)
    (leftCost : State₁ → ℝ) (rightCost : State₂ → ℝ)
    (referenceLeftPositive : ∀ state, 0 < referenceLeft state)
    (referenceRightPositive : ∀ state, 0 < referenceRight state) :
    FEP.CollectiveInference.collectiveVFE
        actualLeft referenceLeft actualRight referenceRight leftCost rightCost =
      FEP.CollectiveInference.variationalFreeEnergy
          actualLeft referenceLeft leftCost +
        FEP.CollectiveInference.variationalFreeEnergy
          actualRight referenceRight rightCost
```

### fep-109: Independent-Agent Expected-Free-Energy Additivity

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep109.FEP109.fep109_independentEFE_additive`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP109`.

Invariant: Expected free energy of product predictive and preference laws with additive ambiguity equals the sum of independent agent-level expected free energies.

Assumptions: Both finite preference factors have full support, predictive and preference joint laws are products, and ambiguity is coordinate-additive. No additivity is claimed for statistically coupled agents, shared outcomes, or a nonseparable ambiguity functional.

Non-vacuity: Distinct predictive and preference factors yield nonzero risk terms, while nonconstant ambiguity functions make both component expectations contribute to the collective value.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep109_independentEFE_additive and fep109_additiveAmbiguity_expectation with preference support explicit.

```lean
theorem fep109_independentEFE_additive
    (predictiveLeft preferenceLeft : FiniteLaw State₁)
    (predictiveRight preferenceRight : FiniteLaw State₂)
    (ambiguityLeft : State₁ → ℝ) (ambiguityRight : State₂ → ℝ)
    (preferenceLeftPositive : ∀ state, 0 < preferenceLeft state)
    (preferenceRightPositive : ∀ state, 0 < preferenceRight state) :
    FEP.CollectiveInference.independentCollectiveEFE
        predictiveLeft preferenceLeft predictiveRight preferenceRight
        ambiguityLeft ambiguityRight =
      FEP.CollectiveInference.expectedFreeEnergy
          predictiveLeft preferenceLeft ambiguityLeft +
        FEP.CollectiveInference.expectedFreeEnergy
          predictiveRight preferenceRight ambiguityRight
```

### fep-110: Unit-Weight Product-of-Experts Pool Normalization

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep110.FEP110.fep110_unitWeightProductOfExpertsPool_normalized`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP110`.

Invariant: The unit-weight finite product-of-experts pool is the normalized pointwise product of two laws and has total mass one whenever its normalizer is positive.

Assumptions: The state carrier is finite and the pointwise-product normalizer must be strictly positive. Disjoint supports are excluded. This unit-weight product uses exponents one, not the one-half exponents of an equal-weight logarithmic pool, and no externally Bayesian pooling property is claimed.

Non-vacuity: For Boolean laws with true masses3/4 and2/3, the product normalizer is7/12 and the pool true mass is6/7, different from both inputs. Disjoint opposite point masses have zero normalizer and are excluded. Distinct overlap alone does not guarantee both inputs change.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep110_unitWeightProductOfExpertsPool_normalized and fep110_unitWeightProductOfExpertsPool_mass with a strictly positive pointwise-product normalizer.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep110_unitWeightProductOfExpertsPool_normalized
    (left right : FiniteLaw State)
    (normalizerPositive :
      0 < FEP.CollectiveInference.productOfExpertsNormalizer left right) :
    ∑ state,
        FEP.CollectiveInference.unitWeightProductOfExpertsPool
          left right normalizerPositive state = 1
```

### fep-111: Consensus Mass Conservation

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep111.FEP111.fep111_consensus_pointwise_mass_conserved`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP111`.

Invariant: The specified doubly stochastic two-agent consensus update conserves combined mass at every state and therefore preserves total combined mass two.

Assumptions: The state carrier is finite and both inputs are normalized FiniteLaw values. Conservation is for the maintained two-agent mixing operator; it does not establish consensus, stationarity, or conservation for arbitrary interaction matrices.

Non-vacuity: Unequal input laws redistribute statewise mass between agents while the exact pointwise sum remains fixed and the two output laws each remain normalized.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep111_consensus_pointwise_mass_conserved and fep111_consensus_total_mass_two for arbitrary finite input laws.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep111_consensus_pointwise_mass_conserved
    (left right : FiniteLaw State) (state : State) :
    FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusLeft left right state +
        FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusRight left right state =
      left state + right state
```

### fep-112: Contractive Belief-Consensus Convergence

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep112.FEP112.fep112_consensus_converges`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP112`.

Invariant: Iterating the maintained strictly stochastic two-agent update halves each atomwise belief gap and drives pointwise disagreement to zero.

Assumptions: State is finite and consensus uses the fixed one-quarter/three-quarter mixing matrix encoded by consensusLeft and consensusRight. Convergence is pointwise for two agents; no arbitrary network topology, time-varying weights, or rate in a global probability metric is claimed.

Non-vacuity: Opposite Boolean point masses begin with unit gap, retain positive gap one half after one update, and satisfy a strict decrease rather than a zero-gap tautology.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep112_consensus_converges, fep112_consensus_half_contraction, and fep112_bool_nonzero_strict_witness.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep112_consensus_converges
    (left right : FiniteLaw State) (state : State) :
    Tendsto
      (fun iterations =>
        FEP.CollectiveInference.beliefGap
          (FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusIterate
            (left, right) iterations).1
          (FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusIterate
            (left, right) iterations).2 state)
      atTop (nhds 0)
```

### fep-113: Coupled-Agent Potential Descent

Family: `collective-and-multiagent-active-inference`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep113.FEP113.fep113_coupledPotential_strict_descent`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/collective_inference.py`; canonical namespace `FEP113`.

Invariant: The maintained consensus coupling quarters atomwise quadratic disagreement potential and decreases it strictly whenever the two input masses differ at that atom.

Assumptions: State is finite and the potential is the exact scalar square of the two-agent atomwise gap under the fixed consensus operator. Strictness requires explicit separation; the theorem does not cover a general coupled free-energy functional or nonlinear multiagent dynamics.

Non-vacuity: Any state at which the two laws assign unequal mass has positive initial potential and a strictly smaller positive quarter-potential after one update.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep113_coupledPotential_strict_descent and fep113_coupledPotential_contracts with the separated-atom premise.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep113_coupledPotential_strict_descent
    (left right : FiniteLaw State) (state : State)
    (separated : left state ≠ right state) :
    FEP.CollectiveInference.coupledPotential
        (FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusLeft left right)
        (FEP.CollectiveInference.consensusRight left right) state <
      FEP.CollectiveInference.coupledPotential left right state
```

### fep-114: Sub-Gaussian Empirical-Mean Tail Bound

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep114.FEP114.fep114_subGaussian_empiricalMean_tail`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP114`.

Invariant: A finite independent family with certified sub-Gaussian moment-generating functions satisfies the explicit exponential upper-tail bound for its summed empirical-mean event.

Assumptions: Observables are indexed by Fin sampleCount, are independent under the supplied measure, and each has a nonnegative sub-Gaussian proxy variance. Deviation is nonnegative. The theorem neither assumes IID data nor asserts an asymptotic law; a positive sample count is needed for the usual empirical-mean interpretation even though real division is totalized at zero.

Non-vacuity: Multiple independent nonconstant centered observables with positive proxy variances give a nontrivial exponentially decaying bound as deviation grows.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep114_subGaussian_empiricalMean_tail and fep114_empiricalMean_definition with independence, MGF, and deviation premises.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep114_subGaussian_empiricalMean_tail
    {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (μ : Measure Ω) {sampleCount : ℕ}
    (observables : Fin sampleCount → Ω → ℝ)
    (independent : iIndepFun observables μ)
    (proxyVariance : Fin sampleCount → ℝ≥0)
    (subGaussian : ∀ index,
      HasSubgaussianMGF (observables index) (proxyVariance index) μ)
    {deviation : ℝ} (deviationNonnegative : 0 ≤ deviation) :
    μ.real {outcome |
        (sampleCount : ℝ) * deviation ≤
          ∑ index, observables index outcome} ≤
      Real.exp
        (-((sampleCount : ℝ) * deviation) ^ 2 /
          (2 * ∑ index, proxyVariance index))
```

### fep-115: Simultaneous Finite-Alphabet Frequency Bound

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep115.FEP115.fep115_simultaneous_frequency_bound`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP115`.

Invariant: Per-symbol empirical-frequency failure bounds combine by a finite union bound into an alphabet-wide failure probability bounded by alphabet size times the per-symbol rate.

Assumptions: The sampling measure is a probability measure, the alphabet is finite with decidable equality, sample count is nonzero, and every symbol has the supplied probability bound. No independence or concentration rate is inferred beyond those per-symbol premises.

Non-vacuity: With one uniform Fin3 observation, target mass1/3 at each symbol and deviation1/2, the three count-deviation events are distinct singletons, each with probability1/3, and their union has probability1. Boolean complementary frequencies can instead have identical deviation events; union-bound distinctness is not assumed by the theorem.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep115_simultaneous_frequency_bound and fep115_frequencyDeviationEvent_membership with the exact finite-count event.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep115_simultaneous_frequency_bound
    {Ω Alphabet : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    [Fintype Alphabet] [DecidableEq Alphabet]
    (μ : Measure Ω) [IsProbabilityMeasure μ]
    {sampleCount : ℕ} [NeZero sampleCount]
    (sample : Ω → Fin sampleCount → Alphabet)
    (target : Alphabet → ℝ) (deviation perSymbolFailure : ℝ)
    (perSymbolBound : ∀ symbol,
      μ.real
          (FEP.LearningTheory.frequencyDeviationEvent
            sample target deviation symbol) ≤ perSymbolFailure) :
    μ.real (⋃ symbol,
        FEP.LearningTheory.frequencyDeviationEvent
          sample target deviation symbol) ≤
      Fintype.card Alphabet * perSymbolFailure
```

### fep-116: Finite-Hypothesis PAC-Bayes Loss-Gap Bound

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep116.FEP116.fep116_finitePACBayes_with_confidence`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP116`.

Invariant: For any finite posterior, a Gibbs certificate whose potential is inverse temperature times the population-minus-empirical loss gap converts a certified log-MGF budget into an empirical-loss plus KL-complexity population-loss bound.

Assumptions: Hypotheses are finite; inverse temperature is positive; the Gibbs reference equals the prior and carries full support; confidence lies in (0,1); and the certificate log partition is bounded by log(confidence^-1). The theorem is deterministic conditional on this log-MGF premise and does not itself prove that the premise holds with high probability over data.

Non-vacuity: Unequal posterior and prior laws contribute a finite KL penalty, nonzero loss gaps enter through the certified potential, and smaller confidence increases the explicit log(confidence^-1) budget.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep116_finitePACBayes_with_confidence and fep116_prior_support_nonzero with positive inverse temperature, reference identity, loss-gap potential identity, confidence interval, and log-MGF bound.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep116_finitePACBayes_with_confidence
    {Hypothesis : Type*} [Fintype Hypothesis]
    (prior posterior : FiniteLaw Hypothesis)
    (certificate : FEP.VariationalDuality.GibbsCertificate Hypothesis)
    (referenceIsPrior : certificate.reference = prior)
    (empiricalLoss populationLoss : Hypothesis → ℝ)
    (inverseTemperature : ℝ)
    (inverseTemperaturePositive : 0 < inverseTemperature)
    (potentialIsLossGap : ∀ hypothesis,
      certificate.potential hypothesis = inverseTemperature *
        (populationLoss hypothesis - empiricalLoss hypothesis))
    (confidence : ℝ)
    (confidencePositive : 0 < confidence)
    (confidenceBelowOne : confidence < 1)
    (logMGFBound : certificate.logPartition ≤ Real.log confidence⁻¹) :
    FEP.VariationalDuality.expectation posterior populationLoss ≤
      FEP.VariationalDuality.expectation posterior empiricalLoss +
        (FEP.FiniteInformation.finiteKL posterior prior +
          Real.log confidence⁻¹) / inverseTemperature
```

### fep-117: Posterior-Odds Multiplicative Recursion

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep117.FEP117.fep117_posteriorOdds_recursion`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP117`.

Invariant: At positive evidence, posterior odds equal prior odds times the likelihood ratio, and a zero-prior hypothesis retains zero posterior mass.

Assumptions: Hypothesis and evidence carriers are finite. Total evidence, the reference prior mass, and the reference likelihood must be strictly positive before forming ordinary real odds. The zero-prior theorem marks lack of posterior support rather than permitting recovery by conditioning.

Non-vacuity: Positive equal favored/reference prior masses and likelihoods3/4 versus1/4 update odds1 to3. A separate zero-prior favored hypothesis remains absent despite unequal positive likelihoods. Reference-prior/reference-likelihood and total-evidence support remain explicit.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep117_posteriorOdds_recursion and fep117_zero_prior_boundary with all denominator-support premises.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep117_posteriorOdds_recursion
    {Hypothesis Evidence : Type*} [Fintype Hypothesis] [Fintype Evidence]
    (prior : FiniteLaw Hypothesis)
    (likelihood : FiniteKernel Hypothesis Evidence)
    (evidence : Evidence)
    (evidencePositive : 0 < likelihood.predictive prior evidence)
    (favored reference : Hypothesis)
    (referencePriorPositive : 0 < prior reference)
    (referenceLikelihoodPositive : 0 < likelihood reference evidence) :
    FEP.LearningTheory.posteriorOdds
        prior likelihood evidence evidencePositive favored reference =
      (prior favored / prior reference) *
        (likelihood favored evidence / likelihood reference evidence)
```

### fep-118: Exponential Posterior Concentration from a Likelihood Gap

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep118.FEP118.fep118_posteriorGap_concentration`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP118`.

Invariant: A nonnegative per-observation likelihood gap yields an explicit exponential finite-sample upper bound on inferior two-hypothesis posterior weight.

Assumptions: Good prior and likelihood weights are strictly positive, bad weights and the gap are nonnegative, and bad likelihood is bounded by exp(-gap) times good likelihood. These are scalar two-hypothesis weights, not a theorem of consistency for an arbitrary normalized model class.

Non-vacuity: Equal priors with likelihood ratio three to one give exact inferior posterior mass one tenth after two observations, so the posterior is positive yet genuinely concentrated.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep118_posteriorGap_concentration and fep118_twoHypothesis_witness with the likelihood-gap inequality explicit.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep118_posteriorGap_concentration
    (priorGood priorBad likelihoodGood likelihoodBad likelihoodGap : ℝ)
    (sampleCount : ℕ)
    (priorGoodPositive : 0 < priorGood)
    (priorBadNonnegative : 0 ≤ priorBad)
    (likelihoodGoodPositive : 0 < likelihoodGood)
    (likelihoodBadNonnegative : 0 ≤ likelihoodBad)
    (likelihoodGapNonnegative : 0 ≤ likelihoodGap)
    (gapBound :
      likelihoodBad ≤ Real.exp (-likelihoodGap) * likelihoodGood) :
    FEP.LearningTheory.twoHypothesisPosteriorBad
        priorGood priorBad likelihoodGood likelihoodBad sampleCount ≤
      (priorBad / priorGood) *
        Real.exp (-((sampleCount : ℝ) * likelihoodGap))
```

### fep-119: Bayesian-Mixture Log-Loss Regret Bound

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep119.FEP119.fep119_mixtureLogLoss_regret`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP119`.

Invariant: Finite Bayesian-mixture evidence dominates every nonnegative component contribution, so mixture log loss is at most selected-component log loss plus its exact negative-log-prior penalty.

Assumptions: Hypotheses are finite, all component likelihood weights are nonnegative, and the selected prior and likelihood weights are strictly positive before logarithms are compared. No sequential regret sum, stochastic calibration, or asymptotic optimality is claimed.

Non-vacuity: A positive selected component contributes strictly to mixture evidence, and a prior below one produces a positive finite complexity penalty rather than a vacuous zero term.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep119_mixtureLogLoss_regret and fep119_mixtureEvidence_lower_bound with selected-component support.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

```lean
theorem fep119_mixtureLogLoss_regret
    (prior : FiniteLaw Hypothesis) (likelihood : Hypothesis → ℝ)
    (likelihoodNonnegative : ∀ hypothesis, 0 ≤ likelihood hypothesis)
    (selected : Hypothesis)
    (priorPositive : 0 < prior selected)
    (likelihoodPositive : 0 < likelihood selected) :
    -Real.log (FEP.LearningTheory.mixtureEvidence prior likelihood) ≤
      -Real.log (likelihood selected) - Real.log (prior selected)
```

### fep-120: Bayes-Factor Multiplicativity and Model-Evidence Update

Family: `learning-concentration-and-model-evidence`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep120.FEP120.fep120_bayesFactor_multiplicative_update`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/learning_theory.py`; canonical namespace `FEP120`.

Invariant: Explicitly factorized evidence ratios multiply, and sequential model-odds updates equal one update using product evidence.

Assumptions: Both reference evidence factors must be nonzero for multiplicativity in the real field. Evidence factorization is supplied algebraically and does not establish probabilistic independence. At a zero denominator, totalized real division returns zero rather than an infinite Bayes factor.

Non-vacuity: Evidence three quarters versus one quarter maps unit prior odds to three, and the separate zero-reference theorem pins the exact unsupported comparison boundary.

Canonical acceptance probe: native Lean compile of fep120_bayesFactor_multiplicative_update, fep120_twoHypothesis_evidence_witness, and fep120_zero_evidence_boundary.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep120_bayesFactor_multiplicative_update
    (priorOdds firstFavored firstReference secondFavored secondReference : ℝ)
    (firstReferenceNonzero : firstReference ≠ 0)
    (secondReferenceNonzero : secondReference ≠ 0) :
    FEP.LearningTheory.bayesFactor
        (firstFavored * secondFavored) (firstReference * secondReference) =
        FEP.LearningTheory.bayesFactor firstFavored firstReference *
          FEP.LearningTheory.bayesFactor secondFavored secondReference ∧
      FEP.LearningTheory.updatedModelOdds priorOdds
          (firstFavored * secondFavored) (firstReference * secondReference) =
        FEP.LearningTheory.updatedModelOdds
          (FEP.LearningTheory.updatedModelOdds
            priorOdds firstFavored firstReference)
          secondFavored secondReference
```

### fep-121: Laplace-Smoothing Error Identity

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep121.FEP121.fep121_laplaceError_identity`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP121`.

Invariant: For a positive finite sample, add-one smoothing decomposes forecast error into contracted empirical error plus an exact target-dependent offset.

Assumptions: The success count and sample count are natural numbers, sample count is positive, successes do not exceed it, and the target lies in the unit interval. The theorem is an algebraic identity, not a stochastic consistency or posterior-contraction result.

Non-vacuity: The shrinkage coefficient lies in the unit interval and the separate boundary witness in fep-122 shows that the offset can be nonzero.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep121_laplaceError_identity and fep121_shrinkage_mem_unitInterval with the positive-sample premises.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep121_laplaceError_identity
    (successes sampleCount : ℕ) (target : ℝ)
    (sampleCountPositive : 0 < sampleCount)
    (successesAtMost : successes ≤ sampleCount)
    (targetBounds : target ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    laplaceEstimate successes sampleCount - target =
      shrinkage sampleCount * (empiricalRate successes sampleCount - target) +
        laplaceBias sampleCount target
```

### fep-122: Laplace-Smoothing Bias Bound

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep122.FEP122.fep122_laplaceBias_abs_le`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP122`.

Invariant: The absolute target-dependent Laplace offset is at most one pseudo-count divided by the smoothed sample size.

Assumptions: The target is confined to the closed unit interval. The bound controls the deterministic smoothing offset and does not identify statistical bias under an unspecified sampling distribution.

Non-vacuity: At sample count two and target zero, the offset equals one quarter and is explicitly nonzero.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep122_laplaceBias_abs_le and fep122_nonzero_boundary_witness.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep122_laplaceBias_abs_le (sampleCount : ℕ) (target : ℝ)
    (targetBounds : target ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    |laplaceBias sampleCount target| ≤
      1 / ((sampleCount : ℝ) + 2)
```

### fep-123: Absolute-Error Transfer through Laplace Smoothing

Invariant: An absolute empirical-error certificate transfers through add-one smoothing with the exact shrinkage coefficient and one-pseudo-count offset.

Assumptions: Sample count is positive, successes are bounded by it, the target is in the unit interval, and the raw empirical error already has a supplied bound. No probability tail rate is inferred in this row.

Non-vacuity: The source error may be positive and the smoothing offset remains visible; the theorem also proves that any supplied error upper bound is nonnegative.

### fep-124: Squared-Risk Transfer through Laplace Smoothing

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep124.FEP124.fep124_laplaceSquaredError_le`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP124`.

Invariant: Squared add-one error is bounded pointwise by twice the contracted empirical squared error plus twice the squared pseudo-count offset, and the inequality integrates under every normalized finite sampling law.

Assumptions: Sample count is positive, each success count is bounded by it, and the target is in the unit interval. The factor-two inequality is conservative and finite-law expectation is not an asymptotic risk claim.

Non-vacuity: Nonzero empirical error and nonzero boundary offset both contribute to the integrated upper bound under a nondegenerate finite sampling law.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep124_laplaceSquaredError_le and fep124_laplaceSquaredRisk_le.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

```lean
theorem fep124_laplaceSquaredError_le
    (successes sampleCount : ℕ) (target : ℝ)
    (sampleCountPositive : 0 < sampleCount)
    (successesAtMost : successes ≤ sampleCount)
    (targetBounds : target ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    (laplaceEstimate successes sampleCount - target) ^ 2 ≤
      2 * shrinkage sampleCount ^ 2 *
          (empiricalRate successes sampleCount - target) ^ 2 +
        2 * (1 / ((sampleCount : ℝ) + 2)) ^ 2
```

### fep-125: Bernoulli Brier Excess-Risk Identity

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep125.FEP125.fep125_brierExcess_eq_sqError`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP125`.

Invariant: Bernoulli Brier excess risk relative to the true probability is exactly the squared forecast error.

Assumptions: The identity is polynomial and therefore holds over real target and forecast inputs; the probability interpretation requires both to lie in the unit interval, which is supplied by downstream statistical uses.

Non-vacuity: A forecast unequal to the target has positive squared excess, while the self-forecast boundary has exactly zero excess.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep125_brierExcess_eq_sqError and fep125_brierExcess_self.

```lean
    bernoulliBrierScore target forecast - bernoulliBrierScore target target =
      (forecast - target) ^ 2
```

### fep-126: Finite-Law Laplace Brier-Risk Bound

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep126.FEP126.fep126_laplaceBrierRisk_le`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP126`.

Invariant: Finite-law expected Brier excess for the add-one forecast inherits the squared-error transfer bound.

Assumptions: Sampling uses the normalized FiniteLaw carrier, sample count is positive, success counts are bounded, and the target is in the unit interval. This is an expected finite-model inequality, not an empirical performance report.

Non-vacuity: A sampling law with mass on outcomes having unequal success counts produces a genuine weighted risk while retaining total mass one.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep126_laplaceBrierRisk_le and fep126_sampling_mass_one.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep126_laplaceBrierRisk_le
    {Ω : Type*} [Fintype Ω] (sampling : FiniteLaw Ω)
    (successes : Ω → ℕ) (sampleCount : ℕ) (target : ℝ)
    (sampleCountPositive : 0 < sampleCount)
    (successesAtMost : ∀ outcome, successes outcome ≤ sampleCount)
    (targetBounds : target ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    brierExcessRisk sampling
        (fun outcome => laplaceEstimate (successes outcome) sampleCount) target ≤
      2 * shrinkage sampleCount ^ 2 *
          FEP.VariationalDuality.expectation sampling (fun outcome =>
            (empiricalRate (successes outcome) sampleCount - target) ^ 2) +
        2 * (1 / ((sampleCount : ℝ) + 2)) ^ 2
```

### fep-127: Concentration-Event Transfer through Smoothing

Family: `finite-sample-risk-and-calibration`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep127.FEP127.fep127_laplaceBadEvent_subset`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/finite_sample_risk_calibration.py`; canonical namespace `FEP127`.

Invariant: A smoothed deviation beyond the transferred threshold is contained in the corresponding raw empirical-deviation event, so any supplied finite-law probability bound transfers monotonically.

Assumptions: Sample count is positive, success counts are bounded, the target is in the unit interval, and a raw event-probability bound is supplied. The theorem composes with concentration results but does not derive their assumptions or rates itself.

Non-vacuity: For n=2,target=1/2,error=0, a uniform Boolean sampling law with success counts1 and0 gives raw bad-event probability1/2 and an empty smoothed event, because the smoothed deviations are0 and exactly1/4. This is a strict-containment instance. Constant count1 gives both events empty despite distinct thresholds; event distinctness is not required by the theorem.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep127_laplaceBadEvent_subset and fep127_laplaceBadEvent_probability_le.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep127_laplaceBadEvent_subset
    {Ω : Type*} [Fintype Ω] (successes : Ω → ℕ)
    (sampleCount : ℕ) (target error : ℝ)
    (sampleCountPositive : 0 < sampleCount)
    (successesAtMost : ∀ outcome, successes outcome ≤ sampleCount)
    (targetBounds : target ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    laplaceBadEvent successes sampleCount target error ⊆
      empiricalBadEvent successes sampleCount target error
```

### fep-128: Observation-Contingent Policy-Tree Recursion

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep128.FEP128.fep128_policyTreeValue_node`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP128`.

Invariant: A finite policy-tree node equals its stage cost plus the observation-law weighted values of observation-contingent continuation trees.

Assumptions: Belief, action, and observation carriers are finite and the horizon is a natural-number depth. Observation laws and deterministic belief updates are supplied by the model; no infinite-horizon or continuous-belief claim is made.

Non-vacuity: A successor node can choose different continuation trees after different observations, whereas the depth-zero carrier has exactly zero value.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep128_policyTreeValue_node and fep128_policyTreeValue_leaf.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep128_policyTreeValue_node
    {Belief Action Observation : Type*}
    [Fintype Belief] [Fintype Action] [Fintype Observation]
    (model : PolicyTreeModel Belief Action Observation)
    {depth : ℕ} (action : Action)
    (continuation : Observation → PolicyTree Action Observation depth)
    (belief : Belief) :
    policyTreeValue (depth := depth + 1) model (action, continuation) belief =
      model.stageCost depth belief action +
        ∑ observation,
          model.observationLaw belief action observation *
            policyTreeValue model (continuation observation)
              (model.update belief action observation)
```

### fep-129: Finite Policy-Tree Bellman Minimum

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep129.FEP129.fep129_optimalTreeValue_eq_min`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP129`.

Invariant: Backward induction exposes an exact finite-action Bellman minimum of stage cost plus observation-weighted optimal continuation.

Assumptions: The action carrier is finite and nonempty, and belief and observation carriers are finite. The result is one finite-horizon recursion, not a contraction or fixed-point theorem.

Non-vacuity: The selected action is proved no worse than every explicit alternative at each node rather than merely being named as an argmin.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep129_optimalTreeValue_eq_min and fep129_optimalTreeAction_le.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep129_optimalTreeValue_eq_min
    {Belief Action Observation : Type*}
    [Fintype Belief] [Fintype Action] [Fintype Observation] [Nonempty Action]
    (model : PolicyTreeModel Belief Action Observation)
    (depth : ℕ) (belief : Belief) :
    optimalTreeValue model (depth + 1) belief =
      model.stageCost depth belief (optimalTreeAction model depth belief) +
        ∑ observation,
          model.observationLaw belief (optimalTreeAction model depth belief)
              observation *
            optimalTreeValue model depth
              (model.update belief (optimalTreeAction model depth belief)
                observation)
```

### fep-130: Optimal Finite Policy-Tree Existence

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep130.FEP130.fep130_exists_optimalPolicyTree`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP130`.

Invariant: Every finite-depth, finite-carrier policy-tree problem with a nonempty action set has a concrete backward-inducted tree attaining the recursive optimum.

Assumptions: All carriers and the horizon are finite and the action carrier is nonempty. Existence does not extend to unbounded horizon, arbitrary measurable policy spaces, or continuous beliefs.

Non-vacuity: The witness is an actual depth-indexed tree and is compared with every other tree on the same finite carrier.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep130_exists_optimalPolicyTree and fep130_policyTree_carrier_finite.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep130_exists_optimalPolicyTree
    {Belief Action Observation : Type*}
    [Fintype Belief] [Fintype Action] [Fintype Observation] [Nonempty Action]
    (model : PolicyTreeModel Belief Action Observation)
    (depth : ℕ) (belief : Belief) :
    ∃ tree : PolicyTree Action Observation depth,
      policyTreeValue model tree belief = optimalTreeValue model depth belief ∧
        ∀ alternative : PolicyTree Action Observation depth,
          policyTreeValue model tree belief ≤
            policyTreeValue model alternative belief
```

### fep-131: Open-Loop Plan Embedding into Policy Trees

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep131.FEP131.fep131_openLoopEmbedding_value`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP131`.

Invariant: Reusing the same continuation after every observation embeds an open-loop action plan into the policy-tree carrier without changing its recursive value.

Assumptions: The comparison uses the same model, belief, and finite depth. Open-loop plans ignore observations by construction; the theorem does not identify arbitrary state-feedback policies with open-loop sequences.

Non-vacuity: At positive depth the embedding duplicates a common continuation across all observation branches; at depth zero both carriers reduce to their unique leaf.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep131_openLoopEmbedding_value and fep131_openLoopEmbedding_leaf.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep131_openLoopEmbedding_value
    {Belief Action Observation : Type*}
    [Fintype Belief] [Fintype Action] [Fintype Observation]
    (model : PolicyTreeModel Belief Action Observation)
    {depth : ℕ} (plan : OpenLoopPlan Action depth) (belief : Belief) :
    policyTreeValue model
        (openLoopEmbedding (Observation := Observation) plan) belief =
      openLoopValue model plan belief
```

### fep-132: Closed-Loop Policy-Tree Dominance over Open Loop

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep132.FEP132.fep132_optimalTree_le_openLoop`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP132`.

Invariant: The optimal observation-contingent finite policy tree is no worse than every open-loop plan embedded at the same depth.

Assumptions: Actions are finite and nonempty, all other carriers are finite, and both sides share one finite horizon and cost model. No infinite-horizon POMDP dominance is claimed.

Non-vacuity: The strict Boolean witness in fep-134 demonstrates that the inequality can be strict rather than only equality under an observation-irrelevant model.

### fep-133: Treewise Expected-Free-Energy Decomposition

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep133.FEP133.fep133_policyTree_efe_eq_risk_add_ambiguity`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP133`.

Invariant: The one-step full-support expected-free-energy identity lifts through every branch of a finite policy tree, and the corresponding optimal values agree.

Assumptions: Every belief-indexed generative model satisfies FullSupport and supplies actual model-derived EFE, risk and ambiguity. The generic update is an arbitrary function and need not be the preceding model's Bayesian posterior. The separate BayesianBeliefModel/update_filtered specialization and its policy-tree/POMDP/risk-ambiguity bridges establish that stronger filtered interpretation under their explicit support premises. All horizons/carriers remain finite; empirical adequacy is not established.

Non-vacuity: The induction ranges over observation-contingent continuations and the optimal-value theorem compares independently constructed minimizing trees.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep133_policyTree_efe_eq_risk_add_ambiguity and fep133_optimalValues_agree.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep133_policyTree_efe_eq_risk_add_ambiguity
    {Belief State Action Observation : Type*}
    [Fintype Belief] [Fintype State] [Fintype Action] [Fintype Observation]
    (model : EFEPolicyTreeModel Belief State Action Observation)
    {depth : ℕ} (tree : PolicyTree Action Observation depth)
    (belief : Belief) :
    policyTreeValue (efePolicyTreeModel model) tree belief =
      policyTreeValue (riskAmbiguityPolicyTreeModel model) tree belief
```

### fep-134: Strict Boolean Feedback Advantage

Family: `closed-loop-policy-trees-and-efe`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep134.FEP134.fep134_boolFeedback_strictlyBetter`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/closed_loop_policy_trees.py`; canonical namespace `FEP134`.

Invariant: In the explicit two-step Boolean model, observation-contingent feedback has value zero while every fixed open-loop second action has value one half.

Assumptions: This is a finite exact counterexample on fair Boolean observations and a mismatch terminal cost. It demonstrates possible feedback advantage, not a universal quantitative gap for active-inference agents.

Non-vacuity: The continuation action provably differs between false and true observations and the strict gap is exactly one half.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep134_boolFeedback_strictlyBetter and fep134_feedback_continuation_changes with both Boolean actions.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep134_boolFeedback_strictlyBetter (action : Bool) :
    policyTreeValue boolFeedbackModel boolFeedbackTree false = 0 ∧
      openLoopValue boolFeedbackModel (boolOpenLoopPlan action) false = 1 / 2 ∧
      policyTreeValue boolFeedbackModel boolFeedbackTree false <
        openLoopValue boolFeedbackModel (boolOpenLoopPlan action) false
```

### fep-135: Finite-Law Measure Embedding

Family: `finite-to-native-blanket-transfer`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep135.FEP135.fep135_embeddedLaw_apply_singleton`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/native_blanket_independence.py`; canonical namespace `FEP135`.

Invariant: A normalized finite law embeds as a weighted sum of Dirac measures that preserves every singleton mass and has native measure mass one.

Assumptions: The carrier is finite with its discrete measurable space. Finite real masses enter the native nonnegative-extended-real measure through ENNReal.ofReal; normalization and nonnegativity are inherited from FiniteLaw.

Non-vacuity: Any nonuniform finite law gives distinct native singleton masses while the full measure remains normalized.

### fep-138: Markov-Blanket Rectangle Factorization

Family: `finite-to-native-blanket-transfer`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep138.FEP138.fep138_staticJoint_rectangle_factorization`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/native_blanket_independence.py`; canonical namespace `FEP138`.

Invariant: Every singleton rectangle of blanket, internal, and external coordinates in the embedded static joint has the exact blanket-marginal times two conditional-mass factorization.

Assumptions: All four carriers are finite and discretely measurable and the static model supplies normalized blanket and conditional laws. The theorem covers the discrete singleton generator and does not by itself state Mathlib conditional independence.

Non-vacuity: The correlated Boolean model contains two distinct positive blanket/joint atoms while retaining point-mass internal and external conditional rows.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep138_staticJoint_rectangle_factorization on every finite coordinate atom.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep138_staticJoint_rectangle_factorization
    (model : StaticModel Internal Sensory Active External)
    (blanket : Blanket Sensory Active) (internal : Internal)
    (external : External) :
    FEP.NativeBlanket.embeddedLaw (staticJoint model)
        (FEP.NativeBlanket.blanketCoordinate ⁻¹' {blanket} ∩
          FEP.NativeBlanket.internalCoordinate ⁻¹' {internal} ∩
          FEP.NativeBlanket.externalCoordinate ⁻¹' {external}) =
      ENNReal.ofReal (model.blanketLaw blanket) *
        ENNReal.ofReal (model.internalGiven blanket internal) *
          ENNReal.ofReal (model.externalGiven blanket external)
```

### fep-139: Native Markov-Blanket Conditional Independence

Family: `finite-to-native-blanket-transfer`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep139.FEP139.fep139_staticJoint_condIndepFun`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/native_blanket_independence.py`; canonical namespace `FEP139`.

Invariant: The embedded finite blanket factorization satisfies Mathlib's native CondIndepFun predicate for internal and external coordinates conditioned on the sensory-active blanket coordinate.

Assumptions: Internal and external carriers are nonempty; all carriers are finite, standard Borel through their discrete measurable structures, and the static model supplies exact normalized factorized rows. The proof identifies authored kernels with native conditional distributions and does not infer the result from zero finite mutual information.

Non-vacuity: A Boolean model has two positive correlated blanket regimes and two distinct positive joint atoms; conditional independence is therefore not obtained by collapsing the whole measure to one point.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep139_staticJoint_condIndepFun and fep139_correlatedBlanket_nonvacuous, plus declaration-level trusted-axiom inspection.

```lean
      FEP.NativeBlanket.blanketCoordinate_measurable.comap_le

      (FEP.NativeBlanket.embeddedLaw (staticJoint model))
```

### fep-140: Conditional Independence under Measurable Coarsening

Family: `finite-to-native-blanket-transfer`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep140.FEP140.fep140_condIndepFun_measurableImages`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/native_blanket_independence.py`; canonical namespace `FEP140`.

Invariant: Native blanket conditional independence is preserved under measurable images of both internal and external coordinates.

Assumptions: The source blanket theorem retains its finite, discrete, nonempty endpoint hypotheses and both image maps are explicitly measurable. The result is Mathlib's composition law and does not cover transformations of the conditioning coordinate.

Non-vacuity: Identity maps recover fep-139, while noninjective measurable coarsenings can merge endpoint states without invalidating conditional independence.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep140_condIndepFun_measurableImages with arbitrary measurable endpoint maps.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep140_condIndepFun_measurableImages
    {InternalImage ExternalImage : Type*}
    [MeasurableSpace InternalImage] [MeasurableSpace ExternalImage]
    (model : StaticModel Internal Sensory Active External)
    (internalImage : Internal → InternalImage)
    (externalImage : External → ExternalImage)
    (internalMeasurable : Measurable internalImage)
    (externalMeasurable : Measurable externalImage) :
    CondIndepFun
      (MeasurableSpace.comap FEP.NativeBlanket.blanketCoordinate inferInstance)
      FEP.NativeBlanket.blanketCoordinate_measurable.comap_le
      (internalImage ∘ FEP.NativeBlanket.internalCoordinate)
      (externalImage ∘ FEP.NativeBlanket.externalCoordinate)
      (FEP.NativeBlanket.embeddedLaw (staticJoint model))
```

### fep-143: Affine Exponential-Family Log-Density Ratio

Family: `finite-exponential-family-dual-geometry`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep143.FEP143.fep143_logDensityRatio_eq`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/exponential_family_geometry.py`; canonical namespace `FEP143`.

Invariant: The supported log-density ratio between two parameters is affine in the sufficient statistic with the exact log-partition difference.

Assumptions: Finiteness, nonemptiness, and strict base-weight support make every logarithm ordinary and finite. The result is scalar and does not assert an extended-real identity at zero support.

Non-vacuity: A nonconstant statistic and distinct parameters produce outcome-dependent log-density ratios.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep143_logDensityRatio_eq for arbitrary parameters and an arbitrary finite outcome.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep143_logDensityRatio_eq
    (family : ScalarExponentialFamily Outcome)
    (left right : ℝ) (outcome : Outcome) :
    Real.log
        (family.law left outcome / family.law right outcome) =
      (left - right) * family.statistic outcome -
        (family.logPartition left - family.logPartition right)
```

### fep-145: Centered Exponential-Family Score Identity

Family: `finite-exponential-family-dual-geometry`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep145.FEP145.fep145_score_eq_statistic_sub_mean`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/exponential_family_geometry.py`; canonical namespace `FEP145`.

Invariant: The scalar score is the sufficient statistic centered by its family mean and therefore has exactly zero expectation.

Assumptions: The centered score is defined on the finite full-support exponential family. The primary unfolds that definition and the supporting theorem proves zero mean. The existing law_hasDerivAt establishes mass derivative=mass*score, so positivity supports the natural-parameter log-derivative interpretation. This does not establish score regularity for arbitrary likelihoods.

Non-vacuity: A nonconstant statistic yields positive and negative centered scores across outcomes while their normalized weighted mean cancels exactly.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep145_score_eq_statistic_sub_mean and fep145_score_mean_zero.

### fep-147: Exponential-Family KL–Bregman Identity

Family: `finite-exponential-family-dual-geometry`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep147.FEP147.fep147_exponentialFamily_KL_eq_bregman`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/exponential_family_geometry.py`; canonical namespace `FEP147`.

Invariant: Finite KL between two members of the same supported scalar exponential family equals the oriented Bregman divergence of the log-partition potential.

Assumptions: The carrier is finite and nonempty and strict base support supplies the logarithmic KL identity. The project finiteKL convention is totalized, but this theorem stays inside its ordinary full-support regime.

Non-vacuity: The companion three-state family has Fisher variance 2/3 at zero while the constant-statistic family has zero variance, so the underlying carrier has both curved and degenerate instances. Equal parameters give the zero-KL boundary by the shared finite-KL separation theorem; this row does not add a separate strict-KL witness for unequal parameters.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of fep147_exponentialFamily_KL_eq_bregman and the explicit full-support theorem.

### fep-148: Natural-to-Mean Coordinate Injectivity

Family: `finite-exponential-family-dual-geometry`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep148.FEP148.fep148_meanParameter_strictMono`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/exponential_family_geometry.py`; canonical namespace `FEP148`.

Invariant: Positive statistic variance throughout a closed interval makes the natural-to-mean coordinate map strictly monotone and injective there.

Assumptions: Variance positivity is an explicit interval premise. The theorem is local to a stated scalar interval and does not assert global dual-flat coordinates or positivity for degenerate statistics.

Non-vacuity: The three-state family has variance2/3 at0, and constant statistics show why positivity is required. A concrete nontrivial interval instance additionally needs variance positivity throughout that interval, derived either from continuity near0 or from full support and a nonconstant statistic; the retained point witness alone is not that interval certificate.

### fep-151: Two-State Chapman–Kolmogorov Semigroup

Family: `two-state-continuous-time-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/continuous_time_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP151`.

Invariant: The exact two-state transition matrices satisfy the Chapman–Kolmogorov additive semigroup law entrywise.

Assumptions: Rates are strictly positive. The entrywise additive matrix identity holds for arbitrary real times; restricting both times to be nonnegative supplies its probability-kernel interpretation. No continuous-path or PDE existence theorem is inferred from this identity.

Non-vacuity: Positive unequal rates and positive times produce nonidentity kernels whose matrix product still equals the transition at summed time.

```lean
theorem fep151_twoStateSemigroup_add
    (rates : TwoStateRates) (left right : ℝ) (source target : Bool) :
```

### fep-152: Two-State Continuous-Time Master Equation

Family: `two-state-continuous-time-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep152.FEP152.fep152_twoStateSemigroup_hasDerivAt`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/continuous_time_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP152`.

Invariant: Every transition entry is differentiable and its derivative equals both the generator-times-transition and transition-times-generator finite sums.

Assumptions: The generator and transition matrices are the maintained explicit Boolean formulas. This proves the forward and backward master equations for one two-state semigroup, not a general CTMC existence theorem.

Non-vacuity: Positive unequal rates give nonzero off-diagonal derivatives away from stationarity and exercise both left and right generator products.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep152_twoStateSemigroup_hasDerivAt
    (rates : TwoStateRates) (time : ℝ) (source target : Bool) :
    HasDerivAt (fun candidate ↦ rates.transition candidate source target)
        (∑ middle,
          rates.generator source middle * rates.transition time middle target)
        time ∧
      HasDerivAt (fun candidate ↦ rates.transition candidate source target)
        (∑ middle,
          rates.transition time source middle * rates.generator middle target)
        time
```
