### fep-154: Exact Two-State Exponential Relaxation

Family: `two-state-continuous-time-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep154.FEP154.fep154_twoStateRelaxation_exact`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/continuous_time_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP154`.

Invariant: The true-state mass of any initial Boolean law relaxes toward stationarity by the exact factor exp(-(a+b)t).

Assumptions: Rates are positive, time is nonnegative, and prediction uses the explicit two-state transition. This is exact finite-state relaxation, not a mixing theorem for arbitrary chains.

Non-vacuity: The benchmark point mass differs from the nonuniform stationary law, so the decaying deviation starts nonzero.

```lean
theorem fep154_twoStateRelaxation_exact
    (rates : TwoStateRates) (initial : FiniteLaw Bool) (time : ℝ) :
    rates.trueMass initial time - rates.stationaryTrue =
      rates.rho time * (initial true - rates.stationaryTrue)
```

### fep-155: Exact Two-State Quadratic Lyapunov Decay

Family: `two-state-continuous-time-thermodynamics`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep155.FEP155.fep155_twoStateLyapunov_exact`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/continuous_time_thermodynamics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP155`.

Invariant: Squared deviation from stationarity decays by exp(-2(a+b)t), and its exact derivative is minus twice the decay rate times the Lyapunov value.

Assumptions: The Lyapunov function is the scalar square of the true-state mass deviation for the explicit two-state semigroup. It is not a general entropy-production or physical free-energy theorem.

Non-vacuity: The benchmark nonstationary point mass has a strictly negative Lyapunov derivative at time zero.

### fep-156: Planning as Inference: the EFE Policy-Selection Bridge

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep156.FEP156.fep156_boltzmann_control_posterior_minimizes_efe`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP156`.

Invariant: On a finite policy carrier with fixed precision, the KL-regularized control objective (expected EFE plus inverse-precision KL to the prior) is minimized exactly by the Boltzmann control posterior proportional to prior * exp(-gamma * G); the minimized value is -gamma^-1 log Z, the EFE splits as risk plus ambiguity, and the finite and treewise argmin views agree.

Assumptions: The primary Boltzmann-law minimization assumes a normalized finite full-support prior and gamma>0 and minimizes expected supplied real cost plus gamma^-1 KL to the prior over normalized candidate laws. Identifying that cost with model-derived EFE is a separate specialization. Deterministic finite-policy and finite-tree EFE/risk-ambiguity argmin statements are separate declarations. No continuous-action or general planning algorithm is claimed; supported sparse-prior and negative/zero-precision boundaries remain separately scoped foundation results.

Non-vacuity: A full-support finite prior, positive precision and nonconstant finite cost give a nondegenerate Boltzmann probability law attaining the unique KL-regularized objective minimum -gamma^-1 log Z. Separately, a nonempty finite policy carrier has a deterministic EFE argmin and the treewise declarations preserve finite optimal values; that deterministic argmin is not identified with the Boltzmann law or its regularized objective value.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all nine fep-156 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep156_boltzmann_control_posterior_minimizes_efe (prior : FiniteLaw Action)
    (prior_pos : ∀ policy, 0 < prior policy) (γ : ℝ) (hγ : 0 < γ)
    (cost : Action → ℝ) (Q : FiniteLaw Action) :
    efeControlObjective prior γ cost (controlPosterior prior γ cost) ≤
      efeControlObjective prior γ cost Q ∧
    (efeControlObjective prior γ cost Q =
        efeControlObjective prior γ cost (controlPosterior prior γ cost) ↔
      Q = controlPosterior prior γ cost)
```

### fep-157: The Perception-Action Loop as a Closed-Loop Active-Inference Theorem

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep157.FEP157.fep157_loop_invariant_surprisal_bound`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP157`.

Invariant: For one positive-evidence observation, outcome surprisal is bounded by perception VFE, whose exact gap is finite KL from the belief to its filtered posterior; the selected filtered-model policy has EFE equal to risk plus ambiguity under FullSupport. Supporting declarations give the finite observation-averaged posterior-KL chain bound, native predictive KL data processing, a Dobrushin total-variation bound under an explicit coefficient, Bayesian-filter/kernel reconstruction and selected-policy minimization. Flat policy EFE makes the policy posterior equal its prior. No realized-observation monotonicity, accumulated trajectory bound or dynamical fixed-point/convergence theorem is asserted.

Assumptions: Policy, state and outcome carriers are finite; beliefs are normalized laws on the finite state carrier, not an assumed finite space of all beliefs. The primary needs positive observation evidence and FullSupport at the filtered model; finite posterior-KL decomposition additionally needs a positive reference law and full likelihood support. The native predictive DPI is separately stated on discrete measurable carriers. Prediction uses an explicit HasDobrushinBound; strict TV contraction additionally needs a coefficient below one and a nonzero initial distance. The flat-EFE posterior identity is algebraic and is not an iterated-loop fixed-point theorem.

Non-vacuity: Nonuniform supported beliefs and informative likelihoods can instantiate the exact VFE gap and the observation-averaged KL decomposition. The selected-policy and filter/kernel equalities are actual finite operations. The flat-EFE case yields posterior=prior, an algebraic boundary; these declarations do not supply a strict realized-step surprisal-decrease or dynamical fixed-point witness.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all thirteen fep-157 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep157_loop_invariant_surprisal_bound [Nonempty Policy]
    (model : GenerativeModel Policy State Outcome) (belief : FiniteLaw State)
    (outcome : Outcome)
    (hEvidence : 0 < model.likelihood.predictive belief outcome)
    (support : FullSupport (withInitialState model
      (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence))) :
    -Real.log (model.likelihood.predictive belief outcome) ≤
        perceptionVFE model belief outcome hEvidence belief ∧
      perceptionVFE model belief outcome hEvidence belief =
        -Real.log (model.likelihood.predictive belief outcome) +
          finiteKL belief (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence) ∧
      expectedFreeEnergy
          (withInitialState model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence))
          (selectedPolicy model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence)) =
        risk (withInitialState model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence))
            (selectedPolicy model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence)) +
          ambiguity
            (withInitialState model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence))
            (selectedPolicy model (filteredBelief model belief outcome hEvidence))
```

### fep-158: Bayesian Model Reduction

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep158.FEP158.fep158_reduction_free_energy_monotone`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP158`.

Invariant: Weights bounded above by one make positive unnormalized masked evidence no greater than full predictive evidence, so its negative-log evidence free energy is no smaller. For a nonnegative mask with positive prior normalizer, the normalized reduced-prior predictive equals maskedEvidence divided by that normalizer; no universal direction relative to full predictive evidence is claimed for this normalized quantity. Supported reduced posteriors obey exact weighted reconstruction and cross-multiplied odds identities. Separately, the Gibbs objective gap to its optimizer equals finite KL, and the fixed-variance Gaussian natural-chart log ratio is explicit.

Assumptions: Parameters and evidence use finite normalized law/kernel carriers. The primary free-energy comparison assumes weight<=1 pointwise and positive masked evidence. Normalized masked-prior/posterior statements additionally require nonnegative weights, positive prior mask normalizer and positive relevant evidence; ordinary log/ratio clauses retain their support assumptions. GibbsCertificate optimizer-gap identities and the fixed-variance Gaussian log Bayes factor are separate statements, with no general asymptotic model-selection or automatic full/reduced evidence monotonicity claim.

Non-vacuity: With prior masses(1/2,1/2), likelihoods(9/10,1/10) and mask(1,0), full evidence is1/2, unnormalized masked evidence9/20, mask normalizer1/2 and normalized reduced-prior evidence9/10. Positive support permits exact posterior reweighting. This exercises both evidence notions without reversing the proved comparison.

```lean
    (hE : 0 < maskedEvidence prior weight kernel data) :
```

### fep-159: EFE and Entropy-Production Time-Scale Separation

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep159.FEP159.fep159_epistemic_gain_bounded_by_affinity`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP159`.

Invariant: For a full-support two-state belief, its finite KL gap to the invariant law is at most productionRate/decayRate. A specific policy-independent perfect-observation model has EFE equal to the semigroup-evolved gap; that gap is bounded by the explicit squared exponential relaxation factor times the initial chi-square expression. At positive time the supported source-target slice production equals(1-rho)*productionRate/decayRate. Under inverse-epsilon rate scaling, the old attainable-scale theorem is preserved. The uniform theorem controls every sufficiently small positive epsilon, and the Tendsto theorem proves the genuine right-hand limit at zero for every fixed positive time and initial finite law.

Assumptions: All statements use the explicit two-state continuous-time semigroup and positive rates. The uniform bound and right-hand limit require fixed strictly positive time but permit zero-mass atoms in the initial law. The affinity and slice-path identities retain their explicit full-support premises; the path identity is one-slice, not a general path-space theorem. The ness_flow import remains only an orphan-adoption marker.

Non-vacuity: A symmetric-rate nonstationary three-quarter/one-quarter belief and a sparse point mass both satisfy the positive-time right limit. The stationary law has exactly zero gap at every positive scale and nonnegative time. At time zero the symmetric point mass retains log 2 at every positive scale, so dropping the positive-time premise is refuted. These controls use the same existing finite probability carrier and public owner.

Canonical acceptance probe: Warning-free native Lean compile of all eleven fep-159 declarations, strict importing consumer with five concrete controls, and seven standard-axiom reports for the two public limit proofs and those controls.

### fep-160: Helmholtz–Ao Decomposition of Nonequilibrium Steady Currents

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep160.FEP160.fep160_aoDecomposition`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP160`.

Invariant: On a finite state space with a full-support stationary law, every rate field decomposes exactly into a reversible part (detailed-balanced relative to the law) plus a circulation part (skew in the law-weighted sense); the stationary edge current equals twice the law-weighted circulation part, the reversible part carries no current, removing the circulation part is exactly detailed balance, the log-affinity splits into the Ao-potential drop plus the skew force, stationarity makes the node divergence of the stationary current vanish, and the directed three-cycle witness is stationary, divergence-free, and circulating.

Assumptions: All statements are finite-carrier algebra: the Ao potential is the pointwise -log p* mass field and is deliberately distinct from the thermodynamic Helmholtz free energy of fep-013; the continuum Fokker-Planck equation, a state-dependent diffusion matrix field, and SDE noise are not formalized. Stationarity and the probability current reuse the established FEP.ContinuousTimeMarkov generator contract.

Non-vacuity: The decomposition is unconditional for every rate field under the full-support hypothesis, and the directed three-cycle exhibits a genuine NESS whose circulation part is 1/2 at the forward edge.

### fep-161: Skew Bilinear Cancellation of the Trace and Quadratic Terms

Family: `geometric-mechanics-notation`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep161.FEP161.fep161_skewTrace_eq_zero`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_mechanics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP161`.

Invariant: A skew-symmetric matrix contracted against a symmetric matrix has zero trace, and a skew-symmetric matrix annihilates its own quadratic form: the two algebraic cancellations behind the manuscript's solenoidal expansion, holding unconditionally on the stated symmetry hypotheses.

Assumptions: Plain finite real matrices indexed by Fin n with explicit pointwise symmetry hypotheses; no continuum Hessian, no smoothness structure, and no numerical witness is claimed.

Non-vacuity: Both cancellations are exact identities on their hypothesis classes, witnessed by any nonzero skew matrix against a nonzero symmetric one.

### fep-163: The Three-Term Solenoidal Expansion and Its Conditional Drop

Family: `geometric-mechanics-notation`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep163.FEP163.fep163_solenoidal_expansion`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_mechanics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP163`.

Invariant: The weighted divergence of the candidate current decomposes exactly into the trace slot, the transport-divergence slot, and the density coupling slot; under the stationary coupling the identity reads tr(Q H) - g^T Q g + (div Q)^T g; both cancellable terms die on the skew and symmetric hypotheses, and the remaining drop holds only under an explicit extra hypothesis (orthogonality or spatially constant Q), with a concrete remainder datum where it does not.

Assumptions: Directional derivative data is explicit (H for the Hessian slot, DQ for the transport-divergence slot); the coupling is an explicit hypothesis, not a solved PDE; the weighted divergence is defined by the exact Leibniz decomposition rather than a discretization of Fokker-Planck.

Non-vacuity: The remainder witness evaluates to exactly 1 on concrete two-direction derivative data, so the unconditional drop is not vacuously unreachable.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all six fep-163 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

```lean
theorem fep163_solenoidal_expansion {n : ℕ} (Q H DQ : Fin n → Fin n → ℝ)
    (g dlogp : Fin n → ℝ) (hcoup : ∀ i, dlogp i = -g i) :
    weightedDivergence Q H DQ g dlogp
      = traceOf (mulOf Q H) - dot g (mulVec Q g) + dot (divQ DQ) g
```

### fep-164: Graph Divergence of the Candidate Solenoidal Current

Family: `geometric-mechanics-notation`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep164.FEP164.fep164_graphDecomposition`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_mechanics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP164`.

Invariant: On a finite node set the node divergence of the skew transport field is twice its row sum; the node divergence of the edge current splits into the transport slot plus the discrete-divergence slot; and the weighted node divergence obeys an exact Leibniz rule whose p-residual does not vanish by skewness alone.

Assumptions: Edge fields are plain real matrices with the flux convention W i j = flux i to j; the weighted statements assume a strictly positive density; no continuum graph limit or first-order asymptotic identification is claimed.

Non-vacuity: The decomposition and the Leibniz rule are exact on every finite node set, and the residual term is generically nonzero by nodeDiv_skew.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all four fep-164 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep164_graphDecomposition {n : ℕ} (Q : Fin n → Fin n → ℝ)
    (g : Fin n → ℝ) (hQ : SkewSymmetric Q) (i : Fin n) :
    nodeDiv (currentOf Q g) i = mulVec Q g i + g i * rowSum Q i
```

### fep-166: Uniqueness of the Law-Weighted Reversible/Circulation Split

Family: `standalone-efe-formalizations`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep166.FEP166.fep166_split_unique`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/standalone_efe_formalizations.py`; canonical namespace `FEP166`.

Invariant: Any competing pair S, A with W = S + A, p-weighted reversibility of S and p-weighted skewness of A coincides pointwise with the canonical symmetricPart and circulationPart of the rate field; the reversible boundary is in uniqueness form (a p-reversible field has the split (W, 0)), and on the concrete two-state datum the constraint-satisfying split is the canonical one with genuinely nonzero forward circulation 1/2 and strictly negative reverse entry -1/4.

Assumptions: Positivity of the finite law is the only analytic input and licenses the divisions; no stationarity hypothesis, no generator contract, and no nonnegativity claim for the circulation part enter the statements.

Non-vacuity: The t-0060 datum with weight law p = (1/3, 2/3) and rate entries 1, 5/2, 3/4, and -1 satisfies every constraint clause, its split is forced to be the canonical one, and the circulation entries 1/2 and -1/4 are computed exactly, so the uniqueness characterization is exercised on nontrivial data.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all seven fep-166 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep166_split_unique
    (hSupport : ∀ i, 0 < law i)
    (hS : ∀ i j, law i * S i j = law j * S j i)
    (hA : ∀ i j, law i * A i j = -(law j * A j i))
    (hSum : ∀ i j, rate i j = S i j + A i j) :
    S = symmetricPart rate law ∧ A = circulationPart rate law
```

### fep-167: Frobenius Least-Squares Projection of the Symmetrizer

Family: `geometric-mechanics-notation`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep167.FEP167.fep167_frobenius_pythagoras`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_mechanics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP167`.

Invariant: For symmetric S the squared Frobenius norm splits exactly as ||H - S||^2 = ||H - symmetrize H||^2 + ||symmetrize H - S||^2, the symmetrizer minimizes the squared distance among symmetric matrices with equality exactly at the symmetrizer, the symmetric minimizer is unique, and the concrete nonsymmetric datum [[0,1],[0,0]] carries residual exactly 1/2.

Assumptions: Matrix projection only, no differentiability, no continuum Clairaut theorem, no Bregman-projection existence/uniqueness claim.

Non-vacuity: The residual example evaluates to exactly 1/2 on nonsymmetric data, and symmetric data attains the zero residual exactly at the symmetrizer, so both the strict and the degenerate boundary are realized.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all four fep-167 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

Exact primary Lean statement:

```lean
theorem fep167_frobenius_pythagoras {n : ℕ} (H S : Fin n → Fin n → ℝ)
    (hS : SymmetricOf S) :
    frobeniusSq (H - S)
      = frobeniusSq (H - symmetrize H) + frobeniusSq (symmetrize H - S)
```

### fep-168: Quantitative Control of the Uncanceled Divergence Remainder

Family: `geometric-mechanics-notation`. Disposition: `formalized`.

Primary theorem: `fep_fep168.FEP168.fep168_weightedDivergence_eq_remainderDot`.

Body owner: `src/fep_lean/catalogue/bodies/geometric_mechanics.py`; canonical namespace `FEP168`.

Invariant: On the fep-163 carrier the weighted divergence equals dot (divQ DQ) g under the cancellation hypotheses, the squared Cauchy-Schwarz budget (weightedDivergence)^2 <= (sum r_i^2)(sum g_i^2) bounds the uncanceled remainder with its abs-product corollary, and equality is attained on aligned nonzero data with norm(g)^2 = 1.

Assumptions: Quantitative budget on the fep-163 finite derivative-data carrier; the coupling hypothesis is sufficient-not-necessary (slot = dot(Qg, dlogp)); no PDE-discretization or entropy-production claim.

Non-vacuity: The aligned datum Q = [[0,1],[-1,0]], H = diag(1, 2), DQ with divQ (1, 0), and g = (1, 0) attains the budget with equality, while the pinned remainderG = (1, 1) datum is strict with gap 1, so both the equality and the strict boundary are realized.

Canonical acceptance probe: warning-free native Lean compile of all four fep-168 declarations.

Source-grounded semantic analysis: exact Lean source slices and lexical roles, joined to maintained review prose. This is neither a certified translation, Lean elaboration, a minimal kernel dependency graph, nor LLM execution. A proposition-looking binder is not classified as a proved hypothesis by this parser.

```lean
theorem fep168_weightedDivergence_eq_remainderDot {n : ℕ} (Q H DQ : Fin n → Fin n → ℝ)
    (g dlogp : Fin n → ℝ) (hQ : SkewSymmetric Q) (hH : SymmetricOf H)
    (hcoup : ∀ i, dlogp i = -g i) :
    weightedDivergence Q H DQ g dlogp = dot (divQ DQ) g
```

Every edge is retained in authored order and with its original kind, rationale, and witness. `formal` and `formal_pairing` remain distinct; a conceptual edge does not gain a witness through this taxonomy. Capabilities and evidence lists are preserved completely in JSON.

- `fep-002` → `fep-014` (formal): The composed theorem expands fep-002 variational free energy by invoking fep-014's composition-product KL chain rule. Actual consumed endpoint lemma: fep_fep014.FEP014.fep014_kl_chain_rule. Witness: `FEPComposed.fep002_vfe_compProd_chain_rule`.

- `fep-003` → `fep-021` (formal): The composed theorem feeds fep-003's discounted ENNReal pragmatic cost directly into fep-021's EFE convention and proves the exact epistemic balance. Actual consumed endpoint lemma: fep_fep021.FEP021.fep021_efe_epistemic_balance. The epistemic balance requires the explicit hvalue premise that information value does not exceed pragmatic cost. Witness: `FEPComposed.fep003_pragmaticCost_efe_balance`.

- `fep-004` → `fep-038` (formal): fep_fep004.FEP004.fep004_fisherMetric_nonneg derives Bernoulli tangent self-metric nonnegativity after fep_fep038.FEP038.fep038_fisherInformation_eq discharges its nonnegative-weight premise on 0 < p < 1; the old metric specialization identifies the carrier. Witness: `FEPComposed.fep004_fep038_metric_nonneg`.

- `fep-005` → `fep-009` (conceptual): The finite blanket foundation proves a normalized conditional factorization and ties each transition row to it, but neither theorem consumes fep-005's finite partition declarations or fep-009's measure-theoretic conditional-independence declarations. A formal cross-topic edge awaits an explicit carrier bridge.

- `fep-007` → `fep-028` (formal): With unit incoming messages and factor exp(-gamma * cost), fep-007's normalized sum-product message equals fep-028's support-aware softmax on the support embedded from the eight nodes into the ten policies, and fep-007's primary message-normalization theorem derives the unit sum of that softmax. The embedding and the exponential factor are explicit in the witness; no general belief-propagation or free-energy claim is made. Added 2026-10-08 (LEAN-8, issue 106). Actual consumed endpoint lemma: fep_fep007.FEP007.fep007_normalizedMessage_sum_one. Witness: `FEPComposed.fep007_normalizedMessage_is_fep028_softmax`.

- `fep-008` → `fep-028` (conceptual): Finite argmin existence and finite softmax normalization represent deterministic and stochastic policy-selection layers.

- `fep-011` → `fep-026` (formal): fep_fep011.FEP011.fep011_surprise_nonneg derives nonnegative fep026 prior complexity through the exact negative-log identity, for probability mass 0 < p ≤ 1; arbitrary real densities above 1 are excluded. Witness: `FEPComposed.fep011_fep026_complexity_nonneg`.

- `fep-012` → `fep-028` (formal): The composed theorem supplies fep-012's entropy-regularized policy objective with fep-028's globally normalized unit-interval softmax law.
